## Summary C — the prophecies of the Messiah

Payne’s Summary C lists the prophecies with personal reference to Christ, with his own catalogue numbers. These are the verses most often cited in Messianic apologetics, in the form Payne printed them.

## Common questions

### How many prophecies are in the Bible?

J. Barton Payne's Encyclopedia of Biblical Prophecy (1973) catalogues 1,817 predictive prophecies across the Bible, occupying 8,352 predictive verses — about 27% of the Bible's 31,124 verses. The Old Testament holds 1,239 of them across 6,641 verses (28.5% of the Old Testament); the New Testament holds 578 across 1,711 verses (21.5%).

### Which book of the Bible contains the most prophecy?

By Payne's count the prophetic books dominate in density rather than raw totals. Every book's figures — predictions, predictive verses, and the percentage of the book that is predictive — are listed in full on this page, taken from Payne's Statistical Appendix.

### What is the Encyclopedia of Biblical Prophecy?

It is J. Barton Payne's 1973 reference work, published by Harper & Row, which catalogues and numbers every predictive prophecy in the Bible and analyses each one. It remains the most complete single catalogue of biblical prediction, and its numbering system is still cited in prophecy scholarship.

### Are these prophecies quoted in the King James Version?

Yes. Bible Prophecy Explorer quotes the King James Version throughout unless a passage is explicitly noted otherwise.

## On the figures

**[Source]** The per-book statistics are transcribed from the Statistical Appendix of Payne’s *Encyclopedia of Biblical Prophecy* (Harper & Row, 1973), and Summary C from the same volume. **[Honest note]** The per-book prediction counts on this page sum to 1,813, four short of Payne’s stated total of 1,817; the difference lies in books his appendix leaves unlisted or in his own rounding, and we have not reconciled it. Payne’s counts are also his judgements — where a passage contains one prophecy or three is a matter of interpretation, and other scholars count differently. We reproduce his figures as his, not as the last word.
